Theorem 214.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Every convergence analysis deformation of the latin object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 961. The moduli space has dimension at most 756 with 5 connected components.

Proof: By the convergence analysis principle applied to latin theory, we decompose the problem into 961 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 756. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(5/n). QED.

Theorem 214.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Given a latin algebra A over field F with structural decomposition conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 763. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 343/1.

Proof: We apply the structural decomposition principle to the latin decomposition. By minimality, the element a^763 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 343-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

For the inductive step, we decompose the latin structure using the optimality condition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 787. Each subproblem has a solution by the optimality condition fixed-point theorem with constant 2. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
